## SHRI KANWAR SINGH TANWAR:

## SHRI SURESH KUMAR KASHYAP:

Will the Minister of FINANCE be pleased to state:-

- (a)  the  salient  features  of  the  Stand-up  India  Scheme  and  the  details  of achievements made thereunder;
- (b)  the  manner  in  which  the  Stand-up  India  Scheme  breaks  barriers  by providing  bank  loans  to  SC,  ST  and  Women  entrepreneurs  to  start  new business along with the criteria fixed for selection of beneficiaries;
- (c) the growth in the total amount sanctioned and accounts opened by SC, ST and Women entrepreneurs under the scheme since 2018 in various States of the country, State-wise including Karnataka, Maharashtra, Tamil Nadu, Uttar Pradesh and Himachal Pradesh;
- (d) the details of the bank loan provided to SC, ST and Women entrepreneurs in the tribal areas of the country including Palghar district in Maharashtra;
- (e) the details of the loan amount, interest rate and repayment under the said scheme  across  the  country,  State-wise  including  Shimla  Lok  Sabha Constituency in Himachal Pradesh; and

- (f) the role of financial institutions in facilitating the disbursement of loans under the Stand-up India Scheme in various States including Tamil Nadu and the challenges being faced in reaching out to the targeted beneficiaries along with the number of new businesses established so far, State-wise?

## THE MINISTER OF STATE IN THE MINISTRY OF FINANCE (SHRI PANKAJ CHOUDHARY):

(a) to (e): The Stand-up India Scheme was launched on 05 th April, 2016 with an objective  to  provide  loans  from  Scheduled  Commercial  Banks  (SCBs) of  value between  Rs.10  lakh  and  Rs.1  Crore  to  at  least  one  Scheduled  Caste  (SC)  or Scheduled Tribe (ST)  borrower  and  one  woman  borrower  per  Bank  branch  for setting up a greenfield enterprise in the manufacturing, services or trading sector and also for activities allied to agriculture.

As on 31.03.2025, a total of 2.75 lakh loans amounting to Rs.62,790 crore have been sanctioned as against the allocated target of 2.50 lakh loan accounts. The basic eligibility criteria under Stand Up India Scheme is as under:

QUESTION 33
================================================================================

## 21.  DR. C M RAMESH :

Will the Minister of CULTURE be pleased to state:

- (a) Whether it is true that  total number of  962 proposals received from Andhra Pradesh for providing pension/financial assistance for artists who have now become old during the last five years i.e., from 2019-20 to 2023-24;
- (b) if so, the details of applications received year and district-wise;
- (c) whether  it  is  true  that  majority  of  applications  have  been  rejected  by  the Government;
- (d) if so, the details thereof along with the reasons therefor;
- (e) whether the Government is contemplating to be a bit lenient towards these artists  since  they  had  contributed  in  keeping  our  culture,  art  and  traditions alive; and

- (f) the details of applications received in 2024-25 and in the first six months of current financial year?

## THE MINISTER OF CULTURE; AND MINISTER OF TOURISM (SHRI GAJENDRA SINGH SHEKHAWAT):

- (a) to (d) : The total number of proposals received, approved and rejected from the state of Andhra Pradesh under the 'Scheme for Financial Assistance for Veteran Artists' for consideration for grant of financial assistance during the last five years, i.e. from 2019-20 to 2023-24 are given below. The districtwise data of proposals received under the scheme is not maintained in this Ministry.

|   Sl. No. | Year    |   Proposals received |   Proposals approved |   Proposals rejected |
|-----------|---------|----------------------|----------------------|----------------------|
|         1 | 2019-20 |                  177 |                   35 |                  142 |
|         2 | 2020-21 |                   52 |                   34 |                   18 |
|         3 | 2021-22 |                  233 |                   91 |                  142 |
|         4 | 2022-23 |                  218 |                   33 |                  185 |
|         5 | 2023-24 |                  291 |                   38 |                  253 |

The  proposals found  incomplete  due to  non-submission of recommendations of the  Art  &amp;  Culture  Department  of  State  Government/UT  administration  or  the concerned  Zonal  Cultural  Centre  (ZCC)  under  this  Ministry,  lack  of  requisite documents and artistic credentials are rejected.

224

(e) : Recognizing the significant contribution by the artists in promotion of our art and  culture,  and  to  mitigate  the  hardships  faced  by  the  selected  artists,  the following actions have been taken by the Ministry in the recent years:

- (i)   The quantum of financial assistance under the scheme has been enhanced from Rs. 4000/- per month to Rs. 6000/- per month w.e.f. June, 2022;
- (ii)  The requirement of submitting income certificate every year has now been done away and instead it is now required to be submitted once in five years;
- (iii) Earlier,  the  applicant  artist  applying  under  the  scheme  should  have  been mandatorily be a recipient of artists pension of atleast Rs. 500/- per month from the concerned State Government/UT Administration. This condition has been relaxed and now also provides for verification and recommendation of the  artistic  credentials  by  the  concerned  Zonal  Cultural  Centre  (ZCC), Ministry of Culture in case the applicant is not a recipient of artists pension from the State Govt./UT Administration;
- (iv) To  ensure  smooth  and  timely  disbursal  of  financial  assistance  to  the selected artists, an MoU has been signed with Canara Bank on 28.06.2023.

The above efforts have led to an increase in the annual disbursement from Rs.  8.71  Cr.  for  2000  artists  in  the  financial  year  2020-21  to  Rs.  26.09  Cr.  for 4636 artists in 2024-25.

(f):          A  total  of  3182  applications  have been  received during the year 2024-25 and in the current financial year, 2025-26 (till 15-07-2025).

## DEVELOPMENT OF GOSABARA-MOKARSAGAR WETLAND COMPLEX IN PORBANDAR

================================================================================
QUESTION 34
================================================================================

## 22. SHRIMATI POONAMBEN MAADAM:

Will the Minister of TOURISM be pleased to state:

- (a)  whether  the  Government  has  initiated  any  plans  to  develop  the  GosabaraMokarsagar  wetland  complex  in  Porbandar  into  a  sustainable  eco-tourism destination;
- (b)  if so, the details thereof;
- (c)  whether the eco-tourism project is aligned with conservation goals, focusing on preserving the wetland's biodiversity while promoting tourism; and
- (d)  if so, the details thereof and safeguards that are in place to ensure minimal disturbance to migratory and resident bird species?

## THE MINISTER OF CULTURE; AND MINISTER OF TOURISM (SHRI GAJENDRA SINGH SHEKHAWAT):

(a) to (d): Development and promotion of Tourism is primarily undertaken by the concerned State Government. However, the Ministry of Tourism under its various schemes complements these efforts by providing financial assistance to the State Governments/UT Administrations for development of tourism projects.

In  the  year  2024-25,  the  Ministry  of  Tourism  issued  operational  guidelines  for 'Special  Assistance  to  States  for  Capital  Investment  -  Development  of  Iconic Tourist Centres to Global Scale'(SASCI) scheme with the objective to

226

comprehensively  develop  iconic  tourist  centres  in  the  country,  branding  and marketing them at global scale. Under the said scheme the Government of India has  sanctioned  40  projects  in  the  country  for  Rs.3295.76  Crore  including  the project  'Ecotourism  Destination  at  Kerly  Mokarsagar'  for  Rs.99.50  Crore  in Gujarat.  The  projects  under  SASCI  scheme  have been  identified based  on  the project proposals submitted by the concerned State Governments, its examination on the prescribed parameters and as per the institutional framework detailed in the scheme guidelines.

As  per  the  contours  of  the  scheme,  the  Detailed  Project  Report  (DPR)  for  the projects is to be formulated by the concerned State Government after analysing all the relevant factors including environmental. Further, it is the responsibility of the concerned State to obtain all necessary clearances which are required as per the prevailing rules and regulations including those relating to environment, forest &amp; pollution, Archaeological Survey of India and other pre-construction clearances before the implementation of the project.

## REJECTION OF EDUCATION LOAN

================================================================================
QUESTION 35
================================================================================

## 23.  ADV DEAN KURIAKOSE

Will the Minister of FINANCE be pleased to state:

- (a) whether the Banks check the CIBIL score of parents and reject the educational loans to their children;

- (b) if so, the details thereof;
- (c) whether the Banks are taking any other form of security like land documents or gold to process the educational loans;
- (d) if so, the details thereof; and
- (e)  the  details  of  benefits which  the  Banks  are  providing  to students  to achieve education as a part of the priority sector lending?

## THE MINISTER OF STATE IN THE MINISTRY OF FINANCE (SHRI PANKAJ CHOUDHARY):

(a)  and  (b):  All  Scheduled  Commercial  Banks  have  been  advised  by  Reserve Bank of India (RBI) to adopt Model Education Loan Scheme (MELS), 2022 (last amended  on  21.3.2024),  formulated  by  Indian  Banks'  Association  (IBA).  The scheme inter-alia provides that if the  Student / Parent / Guardian has no credit history,  they  are  assumed  to  be  creditworthy.  However,  in  case  of  an  adverse credit  history,  banks  at  their  discretion  may  frame  a  suitable  criterion  based  on their risk appetite. For loans up to ₹ 7.50 lakhs, generally banks take favourable view as these loans are backed by Credit Guarantee Fund Scheme for Education loan.  Moreover,  rejection  of  loan  application,  if  any,  shall  be  done  with  the concurrence  of  the  next  higher  authority.  Further,  IBA  submitted  that,  in  cases where credit history is available, banks use credit reports from Credit Information Companies  (CICs)  while  appraising  Education  Loan  applications  as  part  of  the overall  evaluation  process  for  loan  applications.  The  creditworthiness  of  the

21.07.2025

228

applicant/co-applicant is a crucial factor in determining loan eligibility and terms. A favourable credit report generally facilitates a smoother approval process.

(c) and (d): With respect to taking security, RBI vide circular RPCD.SME&amp;NFS.BC.No. 69/06.12.05 /2009-10 dated April 12, 2010, on Collateral Free Loans - Educational Loan Scheme, has advised that banks must not, mandatorily, obtain collateral security in the case of educational loans upto ₹ 4 lakh.

Further,  the  IBA's  MELS  provides  that,  no  collateral  security  or  third-party guarantee  is  required  for  loans  amount  up  to  ₹  7.50  lakhs,  provided  they  are eligible  for  Central  Sector  Interest  Subsidy  Scheme  (CSIS)  /  Credit  Guarantee Fund Scheme for Education loan.

Public  Sector  Banks  (PSBs)  also  provide  collateral  free  loans  beyond  ₹  7.50 lakhs, on case to case basis as per their Board approved policies.

Moreover,  PM  Vidyalaxmi  scheme  has  been  launched  on  06.11.2024,  which enables loans through banks to meritorious students so that financial constraints do  not  prevent  any  youth  of  India  from  pursuing  quality  higher  education.  The scheme facilitates and enables education loans to meritorious students who get admission in  the  top  860  Quality  Higher  Educational  Institutions  (QHEIs)  in  the country and enables meritorious students of these QHEIs to take collateral free, guarantor  free  education  loans  through  a  simple,  transparent,  student-friendly application process.

229

(e):  With  respect  to  providing  benefits  by  banks  to  students,  the  Central Sector Interest  Subsidy  Scheme  provides  full  interest  subsidy  during  the  moratorium period  on  loan  availed  under  IBA's  MELS  for  pursuing  technical/professional courses in India. Students whose annual gross parental/ family income is up to ₹ 4.5 lakh are eligible under the scheme.

Further,  the  PM  Vidyalaxmi  scheme  also  provides  for  3%  interest  subvention, during moratorium period, on loans up to ₹ 10 lakhs to a maximum of one lakh needy students in a year, who do not get benefit of any other scholarship/ interest subvention on education loans and whose annual family income is less than ₹ 8 lakhs.

## SOCIAL SECURITY BENEFITS FOR GIG WORKERS

================================================================================
QUESTION 38
================================================================================

## 27. SHRI K C VENUGOPAL:

Will the Minister of LABOUR AND EMPLOYMENT be pleased to state:

- (a) whether the Government has any data on the number of workers engaged in Gig Economy, and if so, the details thereof alongwith number of Gig workforce, State-wise;
- (b) whether the Government is aware that the majority of the platform-based gig workers work for eight to ten hours daily and yet do not receive any basic social security benefits;
- (c) the details of the concrete steps taken by the Government to extend essential social security coverage to these gig workers, including any regulatory measures or amendments under consideration;
- (d) whether the Government is exploring a reclassification of gig workers to bring them within the ambit of existing social security schemes and if so, the details thereof along with specific proposals being evaluated; and
- (e) the timeline by which the Government expects to implement a comprehensive framework ensuring fair social security benefits for platform-based gig workers?

THE  MINISTER  OF  STATE  IN  THE  MINISTRY  OF  MICRO,  SMALL  AND MEDIUM ENTERPRISES; AND MINISTER OF STATE IN THE MINISTRY OF LABOUR AND EMPLOYMENT (KUMARI  SHOBHA KARANDLAJE):

237

(a): As per an estimation by NITI Aayog vide its report titled 'India`s Booming Gig and Platform Economy' published in June 2022, the number of gig workers and platform workers in the country was 7.7 million in 2020-21 which is expected to rise to 23.5 million by 2029-30.

(b) to (e): For the first time, the definition of 'gig workers' and 'platform workers' and  provisions  related  to  the  same  have  been  provided  in  the  Code  on  Social Security, 2020 which has been enacted by the Parliament.

The Code provides for framing of suitable social security measures for gig workers  and  platform  workers  on  matters  relating  to  life  and  disability  cover, accident insurance, health and maternity benefits, old age protection, etc.

The  Government in its  Budget  Announcement for  financial  year  2025-26 has  announced several key measures for the welfare of  Gig  workers  of  online platforms (platform workers) viz. their registration  on the e-Shram  portal, issuance of identity cards, and extension of health care benefits under Ayushman Bharat-Pradhan Mantri Jan Aarogya Yojana (AB-PMJAY).

## BENEFICIARIES UNDER NSDC AND PMKVY SCHEMES

================================================================================
QUESTION 39
================================================================================

## 29.     SHRI YUSUF PATHAN:

Will the Minister of ENVIRONMENT, FOREST AND CLIMATE CHANGE be pleased to state:

- (a) whether  it  is  a  fact  that  Phase-I  of  the  synchronized  elephant  census completed in the Northeastern States;
- (b) if so, the detailed findings thereof;
- (c) whether any timeline for Phase-II of such census has been proposed in other regions; and
- (d) if so, the details thereof?

THE MINISTER OF STATE IN THE MINISTRY OF ENVIRONMENT, FOREST AND CLIMATE CHANGE; AND MINISTER OF STATE IN THE MINISTRY OF EXTERNAL AFFAIRS (SHRI KIRTI VARDHAN SINGH):

(a) to (d): The  Ministry of Environment, Forest and Climate Change,  in collaboration with the Wildlife Institute of India and the State Forest Departments, has  initiated  the  'All  India  Synchronized  Elephant  Estimation'  in  2022-23.  The data  collection  has  been  completed  across  all  regions  and  all  such  estimation reports are made public after their completion.

## REVISED CRITERIA OF LITERACY

================================================================================
QUESTION 40
================================================================================

## 30.   SHRI RAVINDRA VASANTRAO CHAVAN:

## SHRI MANISH JAISWAL:

## SHRI SUDHEER GUPTA:

## SHRI DHAIRYASHEEL SAMBHAJIRAO MANE:

Will the Minister of EDUCATION be pleased to state:

- (a) Whether the Government has revised the criteria for literacy in the country, if so, the details thereof alongwith the reasons for the same;
- (b) whether the Government has set any target to achieve full literacy rate in the country by the year 2030, if so, the details thereof and the steps taken/to be taken in this regard;
- (c)  whether  the  Government has held any  deliberations with State Governments for achieving the full literacy, particularly with States having poor literacy rate;
- (d)  if  so,  the  details  thereof  and  the  response  of  the  State  Governments  in  this regard; and
- (e) whether the Government has made any specific scheme for the States lagging behind in literacy rate in the country and if so, the details thereof?

THE  MINISTER  OF  STATE  OF  THE  MINISTRY  OF  SKILL  DEVELOPMENT AND ENTREPRENEURSHIP; AND MINISTER OF STATE IN THE MINISTRY OF EDUCATION (SHRI JAYANT CHAUDHARY):

(a) to (e) : The definition of Literacy in context with ULLAS- Nav Bharat Saksharta Karyakram is :

'Literacy is the ability to read, write, and compute with comprehension, i.e to identify, understand, interpret and create, alongwith critical life skills such as digital literacy, financial literacy, etc.'

The definition is aligned with the International definition of 'Literacy' given by UNESCO. The National Education Policy (NEP) 2020 refers to Adult Education and Lifelong Learning at para 21.4, which states that:

'Strong and innovative government initiatives for adult education in particular,  to  facilitate  community  involvement  and  the  smooth  and  beneficial integration of technology - will be affected as soon as possible to expedite this all important aim of achieving 100% literacy'.

A  Centrally  Sponsored  Scheme,  ULLAS  -  Nav  Bharat  Saksharta  Karyakram aligned  with  the  National  Education  Policy  2020  is  being  implemented  by  the Government of India. The aim of the scheme is to support all the States and Union Territories in promoting literacy among non-literates across the country and targets all  above  the  age  of  15  years  who  have  missed  availing  formal  education.

247

Government of India has continued engagement through written communications, regional conferences, video conferences, regional workshops, and review meetings with States/UTs to assess progress, share best practices, and strengthen implementation strategies of Adult Education Programmes.

35 States/UTs are implementing ULLAS and 1.7 crore learners have appeared in the  Foundational  Literacy  &amp;  Numeracy  Assessment  Test  (FLNAT)  so  far.  Four States/UTs  i.e.  Ladakh,  Mizoram,  Goa  and  Tripura  have  become  Fully  Literate under ULLAS - Nav Bharat Saksharta Karyakram.

## FOREIGN AID TO THE STATES

================================================================================
QUESTION 42
================================================================================

## 33. ADV. ADOOR PRAKASH:

Will the Minister of FINANCE be pleased to state:

- (a) whether the Government has granted permission to the State of Maharashtra to  receive  foreign  contributions  under  Foreign  Contribution  Regulation  Act (FCRA) for the Chief Minister's Relief Fund;
- (b) if so, the details thereof;
- (c) whether the Government had declined the request of the State Government of Kerala  for  accepting  foreign  aid  following  the  flood  occurred  in  the  State  in 2018;
- (d) if so, the details and the reasons thereof; and
- (e) the criteria for accepting the foreign aid to the States?

## THE MINISTER OF STATE IN THE MINISTRY OF FINANCE (SHRI PANKAJ CHOUDHARY):

- (a) to (e): As intimated by the MHA,
-  no permission has been granted by the Government of India to the State of Maharashtra  to  receive  foreign  contributions  under  Foreign  Contribution Regulation Act (FCRA), 2010.
-  As  per  Section  11  of  the Foreign  Contribution  (Regulation)  Act,  2010 (FCRA) ,  no  person  shall  accept  foreign  contribution  unless  such  person has obtained a certificate of registration or prior permission from the Central

Government.  Applications  for  registration  or  prior  permission  are  to  be submitted  online  in  Form  FC-3A  or  FC-3B  respectively,  through  the Ministry's  portal https://fcraonline.nic.in .  These  applications  must  be accompanied  by  requisite documents  such as  the  memorandum  of association,  activity  reports,  audited  financial  statements,  and  affidavits/ declarations by office bearers, as prescribed under the Rules.

The grant of registration is subject to fulfilment of eligibility conditions under Section 12(4) of the Act, including lawful existence, engagement in genuine activities for the benefit of society etc.

In  accordance  with  these  provisions,  the  Chief  Minister's  Relief  Fund, Maharashtra,  which  is  a  distinct  legal  entity  incorporated  as  a  public charitable  trust,  independently  submitted  an  application  under  the  FCRA. Upon  examination  of its application and  satisfaction of all statutory conditions, FCRA registration was granted in May 2025.

-  As per FCRA portal, no application for registration or prior permission under FCRA, 2010 had been received from Chief Minister's Distress Relief Fund (CMDRF), Kerala.
-  Further, in exercise of the powers conferred under Section 50 of the FCRA, the  Central  Government  has,  vide  Notification  S.O.  459(E),  dated  30th January 2020, exempted  organisations (not being a political party), constituted or established by or under a Central Act or a State Act or by any

administrative or executive order of the Central Government or any State Government and wholly owned by the respective Government and required to have their accounts compulsorily audited by the Comptroller and Auditor General  of  India  (CAG)  or  any  of  the  agencies  of  the  CAG,  from  the operation of all provisions of the FCRA.

## SASCI SCHEME

================================================================================
QUESTION 45
================================================================================

## 39.     SHRI RAHUL KASWAN:

Will the Minister of LABOUR AND EMPLOYMENT be pleased to state:

- (a) whether it is a fact that as per the India  Employment  Report  2024 (International  Labour  Organization  and  Institute  for  Human  Development), youth economic participation declined to 37 per cent in 2022 and the Periodic Labour Force Survey (PLFS) (May 2025) pegs national unemployment at 5.6 per  cent,  and  if  so,  the  details  thereof  along  with  the  updated  rural-urban unemployment rate in the country, including district-wise in Rajasthan;
- (b) whether it  is  also  a  fact  that  unemployment  in Rajasthan for the year 2020, 2022 and 2025 differs across age groups 15-24, 25-34, and 35+ years, and if so, the details thereof;
- (c) whether  the  Government  has  reviewed  the  impact  of  economic  shocks  like COVID-19 Goods and Sales Tax (GST), and demonetization on employment in rural areas of Rajasthan and if so, the details thereof;
- (d) the details of the funds allocated and utilized under the schemes like National Career Service (NCS), e-Shram and Pradhan Mantri Shram Yogi Maan-dhan (PM-SYM) since 2020 along with enrollment from Churu; and
- (e) the details of the targeted employment initiatives planned for highunemployment districts in the country?

THE  MINISTER  OF  STATE  IN  THE  MINISTRY  OF  MICRO,  SMALL  AND MEDIUM ENTERPRISES; AND MINISTER OF STATE IN THE MINISTRY OF LABOUR AND EMPLOYMENT (KUMARI  SHOBHA KARANDLAJE):

(a) to (e): The official data source of Employment/ Unemployment indicator in India is the Periodic Labour Force Survey (PLFS) conducted by the Ministry of Statistics and Programme Implementation (MoSPI) since 2017-18.

As per the latest available Annual PLFS reports, the estimated Unemployment  Rate  (UR)  on  usual  status  for  youth  of  age  15-29  years  in  the country has decreased from 17.8% in 2017-18 to 10.2% in 2023-24 which is lower than  the  global  unemployment  rate  of  youth  of  13.3  per  cent  [As  per  World Employment and Social Outlook Trends, 2024 published by International Labour Organization (ILO)].  Further, the Unemployment Rate on usual status for youth of age 15-29 years for  the State of Rajasthan has decreased from 13.4% in 202021 to 12.4% in 2023-24.

Also, MoSPI has revamped PLFS from January 2025. As per monthly PLFS reports,  the  Unemployment  Rate  (UR)  on  Current  Weekly        Status  (CWS)  for persons of age 15 years and above in rural and urban areas were 5.1% and 6.9% respectively in the month of May 2025.

As  per  the  latest  available  Annual  PLFS  reports,  the  estimated  Worker Population Ratio (WPR) on usual status for 15 years and above in the rural areas for  the  State  of  Rajasthan  has  increased  from  50.3%  in  2017-18  to  67.0%  in 2023-24 which indicates the increase in employment through various Government schemes/ programmes/ interventions.

21.07.2025

287

Under National Career Service (NCS), since Financial Year (FY)  2020-21 to FY 2025-26, a total of Rs. 375.39 crore has been allocated with an expenditure amounting to Rs. 210.88 crore (till 30th June, 2025). A total of 89,437 jobseekers were registered in the district of Churu, Rajasthan.

Under,  eShram,  as  on  15th  July,  2025,  over  30.94  crore  unorganised workers  have  already  registered  on  eShram  Portal  in  the  country  and  the  total number of registration in the district of Churu in Rajasthan is 4,27,282.

A  total  of    Rs.  1176.84  crore  were  allocated  and  Rs.  479.38  crore  were utilised under eShram scheme.

Under Pradhan Mantri Shram Yogi Maan-dhan (PM-SYM), as on 14th July, 2025,  a  total  of  Rs.  1478.25  crore  has  been  allocated  with  an  expenditure amounting to Rs. 1308.47 crore. 458 beneficiaries are enrolled in Churu district of Rajasthan under this scheme.

Employment generation coupled with improving employability of the youth is a priority of the Government. Accordingly, Government is implementing various employment  generation  schemes/  programmes.  These  inter-alia  include  Prime Minister's  Employment  Generation  Programme  (PMEGP),  Mahatma  Gandhi National Rural Employment Guarantee Scheme (MGNREGS), Deendayal Antyodaya Yojana- National Rural Livelihoods Mission (DAY-NRLM), Deen Dayal Upadhyaya Grameen Kaushalya Yojana (DDU-GKY), Rural Self Employment and Training Institutes (RSETIs), Deen Deendayal Antyodaya Yojana-National Urban

Livelihoods  Mission  (DAY-NULM),  PM  Street  Vendor's  AtmaNirbhar  Nidhi  (PM SVANidhi),  Pradhan  Mantri  Mudra  Yojana  (PMMY),  etc.  The  details  of  various employment generation schemes/programmes being implemented by the Government may be seen at https://dge.gov.in/dge/schemes_programmes.

In  addition,  the  Union  Cabinet  has approved  the  Employment  Linked Incentive (ELI) Scheme to support employment generation, enhance employability and  social  security  across  all  sectors,  with  special  focus  on  the  manufacturing sector.

QUESTION 48

## 44. SHRI HIBI EDEN:

## SHRI ANTO ANTONY:

## ADV DEAN KURIAKOSE:

Will the Minister of ENVIRONMENT, FOREST AND CLIMATE CHANGE be pleas e to state:

- (a)   whether  the  Government  is  aware  of  the  potential  leakage  of  hazardous cargo, chemicals, or oil from sunken or damaged foreign-flagged container vessels in Indian waters, including the MSC Elsa 3 and MV Wan Hai 503, if so, the details thereof;
- (b)   whether the Government has conducted or initiated a detailed Environmental Impact  Assessment  (EIA)  following  the  sinking  of  a  container  vessel  MSC

- Elsa 3 off the coast of Kochi and the fire on a container ship MV Wan Hai 503 off the coast of Kannur, if so, the details thereof;
- (c)   whether  the  Government  has  a  Standard  Operating  Procedure  (SOP)  for dealing  with  environmental  emergencies  resulting  from  maritime  accidents such as oil spills, chemical leaks, and plastic pellet dispersal, if so, the details thereof and if not, the reasons therefor;
- (d)   whether  the  Government  has  invoked  the  polluter-pays  principle  to  ensure that the responsible shipping companies bear the full cost of environmental restoration, if so, the details thereof and if not, the reasons therefor; and
- (e)   the  total  number of cases, such as oil spills, chemical leaks etc. registered under the Environmental Protection Act in the country during the last three years and the current year?

## THE MINISTER OF STATE IN THE MINISTRY OF ENVIRONMENT, FOREST AND CLIMATE CHANGE; AND MINISTER OF STATE IN THE MINISTRY OF EXTERNAL AFFAIRS (SHRI KIRTI VARDHAN SINGH):

- (a) On 25 May 2025, MSC ELSA 3 carrying 643 containers including hazardous materials and marine fuels sank off the coast of Alappuzha. The Indian Coast Guard (ICG) is Central Coordinating Agency for Combating of Oil Pollution in the  coastal  and  marine  environment  of  various  maritime  zones.  Light  oil sheen noticed at the surface has been dispersed by the standby vessel at the site  and due  to  natural  phenomenon of wave  action. Total of 66 containers

washed  ashore  and  have  been  recovered  by  the  agency  appointed  by  the Ship  owners.  None  of  the  66  containers  were  having  hazardous  cargoes hence  there  is  no  damage  or  hazard  reported.  Around  540  MT  of  plastic nurdles  have  been  collected  from  the  Kerala  Coast  and  South  Tamil  Nadu coast.

In  the  case  of  MV  Wan  Hai  503,  the  container  vessel  suffered  fire  and explosions  approximately  44  nautical  miles  off  the  coast  of  Azhikkal  on 09.06.2025.  The  Indian  Coast  Guard,  Indian  Navy,  DG  Shipping  and  other concerned authorities have taken proactive measures to contain and mitigate any potential environmental risks.

- (b) The Ministry has requested National Centre for Sustainable Coastal Management (NCSCM), Tamil Nadu to undertake a comprehensive Environment  Impact  Assessment  in  coordination  with  State  Government  of Kerala for MSC ELSA 3 incident.
- (c) The  Directorate  General  of  Shipping  has  issued  a  Standard  Operating Procedure titled 'SOP to  Handle Marine Casualty 2021', which outlines the response  framework  for  incidents  involving  shipwreck,  oil  spills,  and  other marine casualties. The Indian Coast Guard has developed a National Oil Spill Disaster Contingency Plan (NOS-DCP) for responding to Oil Spill accidents.

The NOS-DCP is a comprehensive tool, which provides a framework of co-

ordination for integrated response by various government agencies to protect the environment from deleterious effects of pollution.

- (d) In the case of MSC ELSA 3, the Kerala State Government has filed Admiralty Suit No. 12 of 2025 before the Hon'ble High Court of Kerala, seeking recovery of ₹9,531 crore from the vessel owners.
- (e) The Ministry has authorised the  State Governments viz.  the Government of the State (represented by the Secretary to the State Government incharge of environment),  Chairman  and  Member  Secretary  State  Pollution  Control Boards  (SPCBs)  and  Collector  etc.  for  the  purpose  of  the  Section  19  of Environment Protection Act (EPA), 1986 to register a case under EPA, 1986.

QUESTION 51

## 50. THIRU DAYANIDHI MARAN:

Will the Minister of CULTURE be pleased to state:

- (a) the details of the specific scientific accuracy concerns raised by the Archaeological  Survey  of  India  that  necessitated  requesting  a  revised  report from the Tamil Nadu State Department of Archaeology regarding the Keezhadi excavation findings;
- (b) the timeline provided to the Tamil Nadu State Department of Archaeology for submitting the revised Keezhadi excavation report incorporating ASI's scientific accuracy requirements;

- (c)  the  details  of  the  additional  excavation  phases  and  funding  support  being provided by ASI for the tenth phase of Keezhadi excavation that began on June 18, 2024, which has already identified six terracotta pipelines and various urban settlement structures; and
- (d)  the  details  regarding  the  coordination  mechanism  established  between  ASI and  the  Tamil  Nadu  State  Department  of  Archaeology  to  ensure  scientific integrity in future excavation reports from the Vaigai river valley archaeological sites?

## THE MINISTER OF CULTURE; AND MINISTER OF TOURISM (SHRI GAJENDRA SINGH SHEKHAWAT):

- (a):  The Archaeological Survey of India (ASI) has not requested for any revised report  on Keezhadi from the Tamil Nadu State Department of Archaeology.
- (b): Question does not arise.
- (c):  Since  2018,  Tamil  Nadu  State  Department  of  Archaeology  is  conducting excavations at the site, for which ASI does not provide any funding support.
- (d): ASI provides permission for excavations as per the provisions of the Ancient Monuments and Archaeological Sites and Remains Act 1958 and Rules 1959 and ensures implementation of the Act. It also provides technical support, as and when requested by any agency including State Department of Archaeology.

## PM-SHRI SCHOOLS IN NELLORE, AP

================================================================================
QUESTION 54
================================================================================

## 54. SHRI PRABHAKAR REDDY VEMIREDDY:

Will the Minister of EDUCATION be pleased to state:

- (a)  whether the Government has selected 42 schools in Nellore district, Andhra Pradesh, as PM-SHRI schools under Phase-I;
- (b)  if  so,  the  details  of  primary,  elementary,  secondary  and  senior  secondary schools so selected out of the above 42 schools;
- (c) whether it is true that the State Govt. of Andhra Pradesh has recommended for 47 schools under the scheme;

- (d) whether it is also true that the Government has selected 80 more schools from Nellore, as PM-SHRI schools under Phase-II;
- (e) if so, the details thereof;
- (f)  whether  it  is  true  that  318  schools  from  Nellore  have  passed  after  District Verification under Phase-I of PM-SHRI scheme; and
- (g) if  so,  the steps taken/proposed to be taken by the Government to add those schools under PM-SHRI scheme?

## THE  MINISTER  OF  STATE  OF  THE  MINISTRY  OF  SKILL  DEVELOPMENT AND ENTREPRENEURSHIP; AND MINISTER OF STATE IN THE MINISTRY OF EDUCATION (SHRI JAYANT CHAUDHARY):

(a) to (g): Under PM SHRI scheme, a total of 42 schools have been selected as PM SHRI schools through the selection process under Phase I and Phase II from the  district  of  Nellore  in  the  state  of  Andhra  Pradesh.  Of  these,  3  schools  are primary, 2 schools are elementary, 17 schools are secondary and 20 schools are senior secondary schools.

The  selection  of  PM  SHRI  schools  is  done  through  the  transparent Challenge Method. A total of 935 schools have been selected in the first, second and  seventh  phase  of  PM  SHRI  school  selection  from  the  State  of  Andhra Pradesh.

## COACHING CENTERS FOR PREPARATION OF UPSC EXAMINATION

================================================================================
QUESTION 57
================================================================================

## 57. SHRI DEEPAK ADHIKARI (DEV):

Will the Minister of EDUCATION be pleased to state:

- (a)  the  number  of  coaching  centers  authorised  by  the  Central  Government  for UPSC exam in the country;
- (b) the details of coaching centers being run for preparation of  UPSC examination, State-wise; and
- (c)  the  action  taken  by  the  Government against unauthorised coaching centers, year-wise?

## THE  MINISTER  OF  STATE  IN  THE  MINISTRY  OF  EDUCATION;  AND MINISTER  OF  STATE  IN  THE  MINISTRY  OF  DEVELOPMENT  OF  NORTH EASTERN REGION (DR. SUKANTA MAJUMDAR):

(a) to (c): Education is in the concurrent list, the State and UT Government need to take proactive action on the matter of Coaching Institutes. In order to regulate the Private Coaching and tuition classes, a number of States/UTs have brought out  guidelines  /  regulations  /  legislations.  Majority  of  these  coaching  centers spread across the country are commercial entities which need to be regulated as per  the  law  of  the  respective  States  /  UTs.  Ministry  of  Education  has  also circulated  a  Guidelines  for  Regulation  of  Coaching  Center  to  States/UTs  on 16.01.2024.

21.07.2025

371

Department of Social  Justice  and  Empowerment,  Ministry of  Social Justice and Empowerment  runs  a  Scheme  namely  Free  coaching  for  SCs,  OBCs  and beneficiaries  of  PM  CARES  Children  Scheme,  for  imparting coaching for competitive  examinations  for  obtaining  appropriate  jobs  in  Public/Private  Sector including  Civil  Services  Examination  and/or  for  securing  admission  in  reputed technical  and  professional  higher  educational  institutions.  The  Scheme  is  being implemented  by  Dr.  Ambedkar  Foundation  through  Dr.  Ambedkar  Centre  of Excellence (DACE) established in empanelled Central Universities. As on date, a total of 19 Central Universities are empanelled under the Scheme.

The  Residential  Coaching  Academy  (RCA)  scheme  was  initiated  in  2009  by University Grants Commission (UGC) to support students from Minority communities,  SC/STs,  and  women  by  providing  them  with  equal  opportunities through  specialized  coaching.  Launched  in  select  central  universities-Aligarh Muslim  University  (AMU),  Maulana  Azad  National  Urdu  University  (MANUU), Jamia Millia Islamia (JMI), Jamia Hamdard, and Babasaheb Bhimrao Ambedkar University-the scheme  offers residential coaching for competitive exams, including  Central/State  Government  services,  banking,  and  private  sector  jobs. The scheme does not charge tuition fees but may levy nominal registration and hostel charges.

Department  of  Consumer  Affairs,  the  Central  Consumer  Protection  Authority (CCPA) imposes penalty on coaching institutes for making misleading advertisements and directs them to discontinue such advertisements.

## NATIONALLY DETERMINED CONTRIBUTIONS

================================================================================
QUESTION 59
================================================================================

## 59.   SHRIMATI MALA ROY:

Will  the  Minister  of ENVIRONMENT,  FOREST  AND  CLIMATE  CHANGE be pleased to state:

- (a)  the  details  of  the  measures taken by the Government to meet its Nationally Determined Contributions (NDCs) under the Paris Agreement; and
- (b)  the  current  status  of  the  National  Action  Plan  on  Climate Change  (NAPCC) and its eight missions?

THE MINISTER OF STATE IN THE MINISTRY OF ENVIRONMENT, FOREST AND CLIMATE CHANGE; AND MINISTER OF STATE IN THE MINISTRY OF EXTERNAL AFFAIRS (SHRI KIRTI VARDHAN SINGH):

(a) and (b): The Government of India stands committed to combat climate change and  has  taken  a  number  of  measures  to  meet  India's  Nationally  Determined Contribution  (NDC)  submitted  to  the  United  Nations  Framework  Convention  on Climate Change (UNFCCC) under the Paris Agreement.

21.07.2025

375

These  measures  include  various  policies,  schemes  and  programmes  launched from time to time, to scale up India's action on both, the adaptation and mitigation fronts.  Appropriate  measures  are  being  taken  for  promotion  of  clean  energy, especially renewable energy, enhancement of energy efficiency, development of less carbon intensive and resilient urban centres, promotion of waste to wealth, safe,  smart,  and  sustainable  green  transportation  network,  creating  carbon  sink through enhanced forest and tree cover, and strengthening climate resilience in agriculture, water resources, coastal regions, health, and disaster management. India's climate action also captures citizens' contributions including those through  the  activities  under  'Mission  LiFE'  (Lifestyle  for  the  Environment) and initiatives like 'Ek Ped Maa Ke Nam.'

The  Government  is  implementing  National  Action  Plan  on  Climate  Change (NAPCC), which is the overarching framework for climate actions.  The  NAPCC comprises of nine national missions in specific areas of solar energy, enhanced energy efficiency, water, agriculture,  Himalayan Ecosystem, sustainable habitat, green  India,  human  health,  and  strategic  knowledge  on  climate  change.  The Government is also supporting States and Union Territories (UTs) for preparation of  their  respective  State  Action  Plans on Climate Change (SAPCC), in line with the  strategy  outlined  in  the  NAPCC.  To  support  the  objectives  of  NAPCC,  34 States and UTs have prepared their SAPCCs to address State specific actions. The missions under the NAPCC have been revised to align with India's Nationally

Determined Contribution (NDC) under the Paris Agreement. The missions under NAPCC are at various stages of implementation.

As a result, installed capacity of solar energy in India has increased more than 41 times, from 2.82 GW in 2014 to 116.25 GW in June 2025. India has progressively continued decoupling of economic growth from greenhouse gas emissions. The emission intensity of India's gross domestic product (GDP) has reduced by 36 per cent between 2005 and 2020.

In  June  2025,  India  has  reached  a  major  milestone  in  its  energy  transition  by achieving the NDC goal of 50% of its installed electric power capacity from nonfossil  fuel  sources;  five  years  ahead  of  the  committed  timeline  of  2030.  This remarkable  achievement  highlights  the  Government's  commitments  to  climate action and sustainable growth to become Viksit Bharat@2047. India's progress is particularly noteworthy on the global stage. Despite having one of the lowest per capita  emissions in  the  world,  India  stands  out  as  one  of  the  few  G20  nations, which are on track to fulfil or even surpass its NDC's goals.

QUESTION 60

## 60. SHRIMATI D K ARUNA: SHRI CHAMALA KIRAN KUMAR REDDY: SHRI EATALA RAJENDER:

Will the Minister of LABOUR AND EMPLOYMENT be pleased to state:

- (a)    whether  the  Employees  Provident  Fund  Organisation  (EPFO)  has  taken  a significant  step  forward  in  its  efforts  to  enhance  member  services  by increasing the auto-settlement limit for advance claims from existing amount to Rs. 5 lakhs in order to help EPFO members in receiving their funds faster, especially in times of urgent need; and
- (b)    if  so,  the  details  of  implementation  and  violations  noticed,  action  taken thereon, beneficiary State-wise especially in the States of Andhra Pradesh, Telangana, Bihar and Kerala along with the complaints received till now and solutions  found  to  address  various  issues  and  other  shortcomings  while implementing it?

THE  MINISTER  OF  STATE  IN  THE  MINISTRY  OF  MICRO,  SMALL  AND MEDIUM ENTERPRISES; AND MINISTER OF STATE IN THE MINISTRY OF LABOUR AND EMPLOYMENT (KUMARI  SHOBHA KARANDLAJE):

(a):  Yes,  EPFO  has  decided  to  increase  the  auto-settlement  limit  for  advance claims from the existing 1 lakh to 5 lakhs, which will help in faster settlement of the claims benefiting the members of EPF Scheme.

(b): No such cases reported.

QUESTION 61
================================================================================

## ASSISTANCE TO INDIAN STUDENTS ASSOCIATION ABROAD 61. SHRI KRISHNA PRASAD TENNETI:

Will the Minister of EDUCATION be pleased to state:

- (a) the list of Indian Student Associations in foreign countries that are recognized by the Government of India, country-wise;
- (b)  the  details  of  financial  assistance  provided  by  the  Government  to  such Associations at abroad during the last five years and the current year, countrywise;
- (c) whether the Government has undertaken any initiative to increase collaboration with  these  Associations  for  welfare  of  Indian  students  who  are  studying  in foreign countries; and
- (d) if so, the details thereof and if not, the reasons therefor?

## THE  MINISTER  OF  STATE  IN  THE  MINISTRY  OF  EDUCATION;  AND MINISTER  OF  STATE  IN  THE  MINISTRY  OF  DEVELOPMENT  OF  NORTH EASTERN REGION (DR. SUKANTA MAJUMDAR):

- (a):  Numerous  Indian  Students  Associations  exist  and  operate  in  different countries across the world. Government of India through its Embassies/Consulates abroad interacts and engages with these Indian Students Associations  for  connecting  with  the  Indian  Students  studying  in  educational institutions in their countries of accreditation. However, Government of India does not accord any formal recognition to these Indian Students Associations.
- (b):  Government  of  India,  through  its  Embassies/Consulates  abroad,  regularly support and collaborates with Indian Students Associations abroad for promoting Indian  cultural  traditions  and  students'  welfare  measures  in  foreign  countries.

21.07.2025

379

However, there is no specific financial assistance that is provided on a sustained basis to such Indian Students Associations abroad.

(c)  and  (d):  The  Indian  Students  Associations  constitute  crucial  pillars  of  the student  outreach  endeavours  and  initiatives  conducted  by  the  Government  of India.  Indian  Embassies  and  Consulates  abroad  maintain  regular  contacts  with Indian  students  and  their  Associations  enrolled  in  foreign  Universities.  This commences as soon as new Indian students are enrolled in foreign Universities. They  are  invited  to  participate  in  Welcome  Ceremonies  organized  by  Indian Embassies  and  Consulates.  Indian  Missions/Posts  also  organize  Orientation Programmes when Heads of Indian Missions/Posts brief the Indian students on challenges  and  threats,  if  any,  that  they  may  confront  during  their  stay  in  the foreign lands and how to take preventive measures. Heads of Indian Missions/Posts  and  other  senior  Embassy/Consulate  officials  also  visit  foreign Universities and Educational Institutions in their respective countries of accreditation  to  regularly  interact  with  Indian  students  and  Indian  Students Associations. During these visits and events, Indian Embassy/Consulate representatives invite Indian students to register on the relevant Government of India portals besides discussing measures to promote welfare of Indian students with  the  respective  University  authorities.  Indian  Students  Associations  abroad are increasingly playing a partner role in the efforts of this Ministry to engage with Indian students studying abroad.

## DPMUS UNDER PM VISHWAKARMA SCHEME

================================================================================
QUESTION 62
================================================================================

## 62. SHRIMATI SMITA UDAY WAGH:

Will  the  Minister  of SKILL  DEVELOPMENT  AND  ENTREPRENEURSHIP be pleased to state:

- (a)  whether  the  Government  is  the  nodal  agency  for  implementing  the  skill component under the PM Vishwakarma Scheme and whether District Project Management Units (DPMUs) have been deployed nationwide and if so, the details of their key roles and responsibilities;
- (b)  the total number of DPMUs or District Coordinators appointed under the said scheme, State/UT and District-wise, along with their names, contact details, and office locations;
- (c)  whether any DPMU or Coordinator holds the charge of multiple districts and if so, the details thereof;
- (d)  the  process  of  payment  to  the  implementing  agencies  in  such  cases  of multiple  charge  and  whether  any  instances  of  double  payment  have  been observed and if so, the details thereof;
- (e)  whether  the  DPMUs  have  achieved  their  performance  targets  during  the current financial year and if so, the details thereof; and
- (f) the mechanism adopted by the Government for monitoring and evaluating the regular performance of DPMUs or District Coordinators?

21.07.2025

381

## THE  MINISTER  OF  STATE  OF  THE  MINISTRY  OF  SKILL  DEVELOPMENT AND ENTREPRENEURSHIP; AND MINISTER OF STATE IN THE MINISTRY OF EDUCATION (SHRI JAYANT CHAUDHARY):

(a): Yes. As per the guidelines of the PM Vishwakarma Scheme, the initiative is being jointly implemented by the Ministry of Micro, Small and Medium Enterprises (MoMSME), the Ministry of Skill Development and Entrepreneurship (MSDE), and the Department of Financial Services (DFS) under the Ministry of Finance (MoF), Government  of  India.  The  MoMSME  serves  as  the  nodal  ministry  and  is responsible  for  overall  implementation  and  coordination  of  the  Scheme.  The MSDE is tasked with overseeing the skilling component of the Scheme and has appointed  the  National  Skill  Development  Corporation  (NSDC)  as  the  Project Implementing Agency (PIA) for its execution.

District  Project  Management  Units  (DPMUs)  have  been  appointed  nationwide covering most of the districts of the country. Their key roles and responsibilities include  generating  awareness  about  the  scheme  among  the  Vishwakarma beneficiaries, informing them about the training dates, batch timings, location of training centres, benefits of the training, stakeholder coordination and facilitating their participation by guiding them to the training centres. Their role also includes regular monitoring of the training centres to ensure adherence and compliance of the training guidelines.

382

(b):  Total  number  of  DPMUs  appointed  under  the  scheme  are  497  (as  on 16.07.2025) who are covering 618 Districts of the country. DPMUs are physically present in the districts assigned to them and also make physical visits/inspection of  the  Training  Centres  to  ensure  adherence  and  compliance  of  the  training guidelines  by  the  Training  Centres.  The  State/UT  wise  details  of  497  DPMUs covering  618  Districts  is  voluminous  and  as  such  may  please  be  seen  on  the website of this Ministry at the link https://www.msde.gov.in/documents (c) and (d): Yes. 107 DPMUs have been assigned multiple districts due to specific project  requirements  and  operational  exigencies.  No  double  payment  instances have been observed as the payments are processed strictly based on the verified number of personnel deployed. The details of DPMUs who have been assigned multiple districts is voluminous and as such may please be seen on the website of this Ministry at the link https://www.msde.gov.in/documents (e): DPMUs are monitored on a task-based review system wherein daily tasks are assigned  by  the  PIA  as  per  the  program  requirements  and  the  performance  is reviewed  on  a  weekly  basis.  Moreover,  DPMUs  undergo  physical  orientation sessions  before  deployment  by  the  MSDE  and  are  further  given  training, assignment and monitoring tasks through various Physical and Virtual meetings by the PIA. They also work in close coordination with Regional Directorate of Skill

Development and Entrepreneurship (RDSDEs)  to ensure efficient  scheme

implementation at district level. Weekly tracking mechanism has also been put in place by the PIA to monitor the tasks undertaken by the DPMUs.

(f): Supervision and evaluation of DPMUs is done by the PIA on a weekly basis.

## MUSEUM STORES IN VICTORIA MEMORIAL AND INDIAN MUSEUM 63.   SUSHRI MAHUA MOITRA:

Will the Minister of CULTURE be pleased to state:

- (a) Whether the Government has a plan to set up cafe and museum store in the Victoria Memorial, Kolkata which has the highest footfall and Indian Museum, Kolkata keeping with their international standards;
- (b) If so, the details thereof and if not, the reasons therefor;
- (c) Whether the Government has a plan to organize festival of Indian overseas in London or Paris or New York as we march towards 2027, the 80 th year of independence; and
- (d) If so, the details thereof and if not, the reasons therefor?

## THE MINISTER OF CULTURE; AND MINISTER OF TOURISM (SHRI GAJENDRA SINGH SHEKHAWAT):

- (a)  and (b): Victoria Memorial Hall (VMH) and Indian Museum (IM), Kolkata are already having Cafe/Museum Store within their premises.

(c) and (d): No. There is no plan of organizing Festival of India in London or Paris or New York during 2025-26. Further, Festival of India is not finalized for FY 202627.

## DENOTIFICATION OF SITES UNDER AMASR ACT,1958 68.  SHRI VISHWESHWAR HEGDE KAGERI:

Will the Minister of CULTURE be pleased to state:

- (a) the numbers of national monuments identified and listed under the Ancient Monuments and Archaeology Sites and Remains (AMASR) Act, 1958 in Uttara Kannada district, Karnataka;
- (b) whether the Government has received any request from the Member of Parliament or State Government of Karnataka for denotification of three sites viz. Kumta, Hosur, Bedkeni from the said list; and
- (c) if so, the details thereof along with the steps taken/being taken by the Government?

## THE MINISTER OF CULTURE; AND MINISTER OF TOURISM (SHRI GAJENDRA SINGH SHEKHAWAT):

- (a): There are 32 monuments and archaeological sites and remains declared as of national importance in district of Uttara Kannada, Karnataka.
- (b) and (c): A request has been received from Shri. Vishweshwar Hegde Kegeri , Hon'ble Member of Parliament, for de-notification of three monuments namely: (i)

The Figure of a Tiger opposite the English School at Kumta, (ii) Carved stones near  the  temple  of  the  Gram  at  Hosur,  and  (iii) Stones  (Viragals)  at  Bedkeni. Notification/Denotification  of  Monuments  from  the  list  of  Centrally  Protected Monuments is a continuous process based on the merits of the case.

## FUNCTIONING ITIs IN TAMIL NADU

## 70: SHRI A. MANI:

Will  the  Minister  of SKILL  DEVELOPMENT  AND  ENTREPRENEURSHIP be pleased to state:

- (a) the  number and details of Industrial Training Institutes (ITIs) functioning  in Tamil Nadu, particularly in Dharmapuri district, including the courses offered and enrolment figures;
- (b) whether the Government has assessed the quality of training and infrastructure available in ITIs, particularly in the said district and if so, the outcome of such assessments;
- (c) whether  the  Government  recognizes  the  need  to  improve  the  quality  and relevance  of  training  in  ITIs,  particularly  in  the  said  district  and  if  so,  the specific steps taken/being taken in this regard;
- (d) whether the Government proposes to establish any new ITIs at the Block or Panchayat level in Tamil Nadu, particularly in the said district and if so, the details thereof;
- (e) whether  any  specific  measures  have  been  taken  by  the  Government  to upgrade training standards, ensure industry linkage, and enhance employability of ITI graduates in Tamil Nadu, especially in the said district, and
- (f) if so, the details thereof?

THE  MINISTER  OF  STATE  OF  THE  MINISTRY  OF  SKILL  DEVELOPMENT AND ENTREPRENEURSHIP; AND MINISTER OF STATE IN THE MINISTRY OF EDUCATION (SHRI JAYANT CHAUDHARY):

422

(a) :  The  Directorate  General  of  Training  (DGT),  under  the  Ministry  of  Skill Development and Entrepreneurship (MSDE) implements the Craftsmen Training Scheme  (CTS)  through  a  country  wide  network  of  Industrial  Training  Institutes (ITIs) for the youth of the country.

At present, there are 14,615 ITIs established in the country, out of which, 3,316 are Government and 11,299 are Private ITIs.

In  the  State  of  Tamil  Nadu,  a  total  of  456  ITIs  are  currently  operational,  out  of which 93 are Government and 363 are Private ITIs. In the Dharmapuri district, 3 ITIs are functioning (Government -1 and Private - 2).

District wise enrolment of trainees in ITIs in Tamil Nadu (including Dharmapuri) in the  last  three  academic  sessions  (2022-23  to  2024-25)  in  ITIs  is  given  in  the enclosed Statement- I .

The List of courses running under CTS in ITIs in Tamil Nadu and in Dharmapuri district is given in the enclosed Statement- II .

(b) :  Establishment  and  day  to  day  administration  of  ITIs  comes  under  the jurisdiction  of  respective  State  Government  and  UT  Administration,  while  the policies  such  as  setting  the  criterion  for  affiliation,  conducting  the  examination along with certification and designing curriculum are the responsibilities of Central Government.

423

Respective State Government conducts periodic joint- inspections of  the ITIs in coordination  with  Central  Government.  Based  on  which  corrective  actions  are initiated  and  the  ITIs  that  fail  to  comply with  the  prescribed affiliation  norms  are de-affiliated.  This  ensures that only ITIs maintaining the necessary standards in infrastructure, faculty qualifications, curriculum implementation, and training delivery continue to operate.

In Dharmapuri district, some units affiliated with ITIs were de-affiliated in January 2025 due to absence of admissions over the last six academic sessions (20182023), indicating underperformance and lack of demand.

(c) : Improving the quality of training in ITIs is a continuous process. Towards this, inter-alia, the standards and norms of affiliation of ITIs are reviewed periodically. The  curriculum  of  courses  under  CTS  is  also  updated  regularly  in  consultation with industries to incorporate the latest technological advancements and industry requirements, ensuring that the skills imparted to trainees remain relevant.

Apart from these, DGT, had allocated funds under the following Schemes/Projects for upgradation of ITIs across the country including Tamil Nadu.

- i. Skill  Strengthening  for  Industrial  Value  Enhancement  (STRIVE):  Under  the STRIVE scheme, financial assistance was provided to State Governments to improve  the  relevance  and  efficiency  of  skill  development  imparted  through

Industrial Training  Institutes (ITIs)  and apprenticeship training. A  total of  500 ITIs were selected across the country, including 32 ITIs from the State of Tamil Nadu. The scheme ended on 31st May 2024, and a total financial assistance of Rs 37.82 crore was provided to the State of Tamil Nadu under this scheme. No ITI from Dharmapuri district was covered under this scheme.

- ii. Upgradation of Government ITIs into Model ITIs scheme: Under this scheme, support was extended to 35 Government ITIs across 29 States/UTs, including Tamil  Nadu.  The  scheme  ended  on  31st  March  2024,  and  a  total  financial assistance of Rs. 3.99 crore was provided to the State of Tamil Nadu for the upgradation  of  ITI  Coimbatore  under  the  Model  ITI  scheme.  No  ITI  from Dharmapuri district was covered under this scheme.

In  addition,  the  Union  Cabinet  has  also  approved  the  National  Scheme  for  ITI Upgradation and the setting up of National Centres of Excellence for Skilling to upgrade ITIs by  improving  the quality  of  training,  aligning  courses  with  industry needs, and promoting industry-led governance.

(d) :  Establishment  of  ITIs  including  start  of  new  courses  comes  under  the jurisdiction of respective State Government, whenever a proposal is received from the States/UTs for the setting up of new ITIs, it is examined and decided as per the affiliation standards and norms.

21.07.2025

425

(e) and (f)  :  Several state  governments  including Tamil  Nadu have collaborated with industries  to transform  the Government ITIs into technology centres / hubs with modern  infrastructure and  training facilities to meet  the  industry 4.0 standards. These collaborations aim to equip Government ITIs with cutting-edge technology  and  industry-aligned  curriculum  to  bridge  the  skill  gap  and  prepare students for the demands of the evolving industrial landscape.

Further,  DGT  has  also  undertaken  the  following  initiatives  to  enhance  the employability of ITI trainees across the country including in Tamil Nadu.

- i. The  curriculum of courses under CTS  is also updated  regularly in consultation with industries to incorporate the latest technological advancements and industry requirements, ensuring that the skills imparted to trainees remain relevant.
- ii. Each course under the CTS includes a compulsory subject on Employability Skills. This module focuses on essential skills such as behaviour, communication,  IT  proficiency, work  ethics, and  other attributes that enhance a trainee's ability to effectively work within a team and thrive in a professional environment.
- iii. Dual System of Training (DST) has been introduced, wherein a significant portion of the training is hands-on and conducted within the industry itself. Under  the  DST  framework,  On-the-Job  Training  (OJT)  is  provided  for  a period of 6 to 12 months within industries for courses with a total duration of

one or two years. This immersive approach integrates theoretical learning in ITIs with practical, real-world experience in industrial settings.

In the State of Tamil Nadu, DST is currently being implemented in 15 ITIs.

## िश±ा मंýालय म¤ राºय मंýी; तथा उ°र पूवê ±ेý िवकास मंýालय म¤ राºय मंýी (डॉ. सुकाÆ  त मजूमदार):
